Section 11:  Plan Development 
 
Describe the involvement of the Chief Local Elected Official, the Local Workforce 
Investment Board and stakeholders in the development of the plan.   Describe the process 
used to make the Plan available to the public, as well as key stakeholders, and the 
outcome resulting from review of public comments.   
a. Describe the involvement of the Chief Local Elected Official (CLEO), the Local 
Workforce Investment Board and stakeholders in the development of the plan. 
The Local Workforce Development Board Plan 2017-2021 was developed according to 
the requirements of Section 108 and the state policy and Workforce Arizona Council.  
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors has designated the Local Workforce 
Development Board to oversee the development and implementation of the Local 
Workforce Development Board four-year plan. 
 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors serves as the Chief Elected Official and is the 
grant recipient, administrative entity, and fiscal agent, in coordination with the Local 
Workforce Development Board.  As the grant recipient, the Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors is required to authorize the local board’s intergovernmental agreements. 
 
On November 9 the Local Workforce Development Board briefed the Chief Elected 
Official on the CEO/Board Agreement, One-Stop Operator, as well as the partners MOU, 
and the upcoming One-Stop Operator competitive selection process.   
 
The Local Workforce Development Board Plan 2017-2021 was prepared by the staff as 
specified in the requirements of the Act.  Stakeholders were involved in the 
development and notified of the public comment that was published in the Navajo, 
Apache, and Gila Counties newspapers.  The Local Workforce Development Board Plan 
will be forwarded to the Workforce Arizona Council for approval. 
b. A description of the process used by the local board, consistent with subsection (d), to 
provide an opportunity for public comment, including comment by representatives of 
businesses and comment by representatives of labor organizations, and input into the 
development of the local plan, prior to submission of the plan. 
 
The Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act Section 108 (d) says:  
  
Prior to the date on which the local board submits a local plan under this section, the 
local board shall— 
(1) make available copies of a proposed local plan to the public through electronic and 
other means, such as public hearings and local news media; 
(2) allow members of the public, including representatives of business, representatives of

<!-- page 478 -->
70 
 
labor organizations, and representatives of education to submit to the local board 
comments on the proposed local plan, not later than the end of the 30-day period 
beginning on the date on which the proposed local plan is made available; and (3) 
include with the local plan submitted to the Governor under this section any such 
comments that represent disagreement with the plan. 
 
The Local Workforce Development Board has included core program and required 
partners in the preparation of this plan from beginning to end.   In addition, economic 
development has been a voice in the conversation to ensure business and industry 
needs and expectations are included.   Staff has been also included in the preparation of 
the plan.    
 
Following individual core program partner meetings to discuss unique features and 
benefits of each funding stream, the partner group met to discuss similarities and 
differences across services.   The draft plan was provided to the partners, Local 
Workforce Development Board members, and Chief Elected Official for review during 
the 30 day public notice.    The formal process will include forwarding an email notice to 
a website link targeting core program and required partners, Local Workforce 
Development Board members, Chief Elected Official, community stakeholders, and 
providers.     In addition, a notice will be posted in the ARIZONA@WORK Job Center to 
alert clients and customers.   Next, a notice will be placed one time in a news publication 
in Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties.     
 
Following the thirty-day (30) comment period, the partners will discuss comments and 
respond with revisions to the plan.  The final Local Workforce Development Board Plan 
will be submitted to the Workforce Arizona Council before April 1, 2017.

One-Stop System 
(Draft) 
Memorandum of Understanding 
With the 
Northeastern Arizona Innovative Workforce Solutions 
Workforce Development Board 
 
 
 
I. Purpose of Memorandum of Understanding 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (hereinafter “MOU”) is to define the roles, 
responsibilities, and expectations to each partner as mutually agreed by the 
parties for the operation of the Arizona @ Work Northeastern Arizona 
Innovative Workforce Solutions (NEAZIWS) Local Workforce Development 
One-Stop Service delivery system in Navajo, Apache, and Gila counties as 
required in the Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act (WIOA). 
 
A. WIOA Section 121(c)(1) and §678.500(a) requires that the Local 
Workforce Development Board (LWDB), with the agreement of the chief 
elected official, shall develop and enter into a memorandum of 
understanding (between the local board and the one-stop Partners), with 
the agencies that serve as Partners in the ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS 
One-Stop Delivery System that is operated in Navajo, Apache, and Gila 
counties. 
 
B. WIOA Section 121(b)(1)(A)(iii) and §678.420(c) mandates all required 
Partners in a local area to enter into a memorandum of understanding 
with the LWDB, relating the operation of the One-Stop system in the 
respective area pursuant to WIOA Section 121(c) and §678.500(d). 
 
C. WIOA Section 121(b)(2) and §678.410(a) describes how entities that 
provide programs other than those required under WIOA Section 
121(b)(1)(B) may participate in a local area – ARIZONA@WORK 
NEAZIWS One-Stop Delivery System as “additional partners” and carry 
out workforce development services available under their program 
through the ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS One-Stop Delivery System. 
 
D. Per WIOA Section 121(b)(2)(A) both required and additional Partners 
are included as parties to the Memorandum of Understanding. 
Therefore, all entities that participate in ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS 
One- Stop Delivery System as Partners, whether required or additional, 
must be parties to this MOU and must abide by the terms prescribed 
herein and by all applicable federal, state, and local rules, plans, and 
policies as applicable and authorized under the WIOA law and federal 
guidelines.

<!-- page 499 -->
91 
 
E. WIOA Section 121(b)(1) identifies the federal programs and requires 
that the services and activities under each of those programs must be 
made available through each local area – ARIZONA@WORK 
NEAZIWS One-Stop Delivery System. The entities that receive the 
federal funds for each of these programs and/or have the responsibility 
to administer the respective programs in the area are required partners 
under WIOA Section 121(b)(1)(B). 
 
F.  WIOA Section 121(b)(1)(A)(iv) and §678.420(d) indicate that the 
requirements of each partner’s authorizing legislation continue to apply 
under the ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS One-Stop Delivery System and 
that participation in the ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS One-Stop Delivery 
System is in addition to other requirements applicable to each Partner’s 
program under each authorizing law. 
 
G. The Department of Labor (DOL) is the Federal agency accountable for 
the administration of the workforce development programs. 
 
H. The DOL identifies the ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS Local Workforce 
Development Board as the entity accountable for the administration and 
management of workforce development and employment-related 
programs in NEAZIWS. 
 
II.  MOU Partners 
 
Per Section 121(b)(1) of the Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act and 
§678.400 the following entitles are core Partners in the One-Stop Delivery 
System: 
 
Title I Adult, Dislocated Worker, and Youth Programs 
Title II:  Adult Education and Literacy 
Title III: Wagner-Peyser Programs and Veterans Program 
Title IV.: Vocational Rehabilitation 
Additional partners with the one-stop delivery system are: 
Community Action Program 
Home Weatherization/Housing Rehabilitation 
Section 8 Housing 
Gila Employment and Special Training 
 
III. Purpose of the Agreement 
 
 
The Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act of 2014 (WIOA) is an 
affirmation of the work that has been done in NEAZIWS to build the workforce 
development system. The foundation of the act is its One-Stop Customer

<!-- page 500 -->
92 
 
Service Delivery System. The One-Stop Customer Service Delivery System 
brings together workforce development, educational, and other human 
resource services in a seamless customer-focused service delivery network 
that enhances access to the programs' services and improves long-term 
employment outcomes for individuals receiving assistance. One-Stop partners 
administer separately funded programs as a set of integrated streamlined 
services to customers. 
 
 
The One-Stop Delivery System assures coordination between the activities 
authorized in WIOA. 
 
 
The purpose of this Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is to describe the 
cooperative workforce training, employment, and economic development 
efforts of ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS and their partners and the actions to 
be taken by each to assure the coordination of their efforts in accordance with 
the State issued requirements in order to establish and maintain an effective 
and successful “One-Stop” Delivery System. 
 
 
This agreement is intended to coordinate resources, prevent duplication of 
effort, and ensure the effective and efficient delivery of workforce services in 
Navajo, Apache, and Gila counties. In addition, this agreement will establish 
joint processes and procedures that will enable the Partners to integrate with 
the current One-Stop Service Delivery System. This will result in a seamless 
and comprehensive array of education, job training, and other workforce 
development services to person with barriers and disabilities within Navajo, 
Apache, and Gila counties. 
 
 
The parties to this document agree to coordinate and perform the activities and 
services described herein within the scope of legislative requirements 
governing the parties’ respective programs, services, and agencies. 
 
 
The ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS Local Workforce Development Board 
enters into this agreement to ensure that the principles outlined in this MOU 
are consistent with the Arizona Workforce Development Plan 2016-2020 and 
State Administrative Policies. 
 
A. Agreement Period 
 
WIOA Section 121(c)(2)(A)(v) and §678.500(5) and §678.500(6) 
requires that the MOU indicates the duration of the agreement,

<!-- page 501 -->
93 
 
amending procedures, and assurance that the memorandum will be 
reviewed. 
 
This MOU will commence on July 1, 2017 and shall remain in effect 
until June 30, 2020. 
 
The partners agree to review this Memorandum of Understanding 
every three years to ensure appropriate funding and delivery of 
services. Modifications will be made as deemed necessary and as 
agreed upon by the Partners. 
 
The partners understand that integration to the One-Stop Delivery 
System is dependent on everyone’s good faith integration efforts to 
improve and provide seamless services to the community. In the event 
that it becomes necessary for any of the Partners to cease being a 
party to this MOU, the said partner shall notify the other parties, in 
writing, ninety (90) days in advance of that intention. The other 
partners shall then determine how to replace or offset the loss of 
participation and resources to the One-Stop Customer Service Delivery 
System and its clients. Termination by one of the partners to this MOU 
does not alter the terms or obligations of the other partners to this 
MOU. 
 
B. Amendment 
 
1.  This MOU may be amended upon mutual agreement of the parties 
if it is not consistent with federal, state, or local laws, regulations, 
rules, plans, or policies or for one or more of the following reasons: 
 
a.  The addition or removal of a partner from this MOU. 
b.  Removal or addition of program responsibilities for any partner 
that administers more than one federal program. 
c.  An extension of the effective ending date of the Agreement 
Period. 
d.  A change in the ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS Operator or 
Fiscal 
Agent or a change in the physical location. 
e.  A change in the services, referral method, and service delivery 
methods currently utilized, methods to allocate costs, or 
methods to determine fair share. 
 
2.  All parties agree that amendments for the reasons listed in part A of 
Section 3 of Amendment need only be signed by authorized 
representatives of the LWDB, the Local Board Executive Director, 
and the affected partners(s).  Amendments for the reasons listed in 
all other paragraphs of this section or for any changes that will

<!-- page 502 -->
94 
 
affect the responsibilities of all parties, require the signatures of all 
parties. All amendments will involve the following process; 
 
a.  The party seeking an amendment will submit a written 
request to the LWDB that includes: 
1. The requesting party’s name 
2. The reason(s) for the amendment request 
3. Each section of this MOU that will require revision 
4. The desired date for the amendment to be effective 
5. The signature of the requesting party’s authorized 
representative 
b.  If the request is approved, the LWDB will notify the 
remaining parties of the intent to amend and will provide 
each remaining party thirty (30) days from the date of the 
notice (unless another timeframe is specified in the notice) 
to review the anticipated changes and to submit a 
response to LWDB.  Failure by a party to respond within 
the prescribed timeframe will be deemed as that party’s 
approval of the proposed changes. 
c.  In the Event that a remaining party has questions and/or 
concerns regarding the proposed amendment, the party 
must list its questions and/or concerns in writing and 
submit the list to LWDB within the specified timeframe. 
d.  LWDB will review the listed questions/concerns and will 
issue a response within fifteen (15) days of the receipt of 
the list. If the LWDB deems it necessary, the listed 
questions/concerns will be sent to all other parties and/or a 
meeting with all parties will be scheduled to discuss the 
proposed changes and to achieve consensus on a final 
amendment draft. 
e.  The final approved amendment draft will be signed by 
authorized representatives of the affected partner then 
submitted to LWDB for the final signature. 
f. LWDB will distribute copies of the fully executed 
amendment to all parties and to the Local Executive 
Director as the MOU oversight agency upon execution. 
 
3.  This writing constitutes to the entire agreement among the parties 
with respect to each party’s role and responsibility in the One-Stop 
Delivery System. All parties agree that any amendment to any 
applicable laws or regulations cited herein will result in the 
correlative modification of this MOU without necessitating a formal, 
written amendment. 
 
4.  All parties agree to communicate details of the amendments to their 
respective staff members whose responsibilities may be impacted

<!-- page 503 -->
95 
 
by changes and further agree to ensure that their respective staff 
members are referencing or utilizing the most current version of the 
MOU. 
 
5.  Amendments that will require the signature of all parties must be 
executed no later than ninety (90) days prior to the end of the MOU 
period and amendments that require only the signature of the 
LWDB, the Local Executive Director, and the affected parties must 
be executed no later than 45 days from the end of the current Fiscal 
year. 
 
 
IV. Visions and Goals 
 
Vision and Mission Statement 
 
The LWDB and CEO have a vision to build economic success for business 
and workers through workforce development partnerships. 
 
The Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce Development Board will work to 
facilitate business retention and growth by partnering with education, 
service agencies and the business community to empower individuals with 
skills that promote self-sufficiency. 
 
The partners agree to share accountability for achievement of NEAZIWS 
Workforce Development Board’s performance standards and commit to the 
vision and goals for the one-stop system as stipulated in the agreement. 
 
WIOA specifies each required partner will work collaboratively to provide a 
higher level of service quality through the delivery services in a ‘shared service 
model”. 
 
The core partners are: 
 
Title I Adult, Dislocated Worker, and Youth Programs 
Title II Adult Education and Literacy 
Title III Wagner-Peyser Programs and Veterans Services 
Title IV Vocational Rehabilitation 
 
This approach will foster system partnerships that build and sustain a shared 
vision and strategy that promotes seamless delivery of services that benefit 
Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties’ communities. This improved framework 
will enhance the development of a more informed, educated, and skilled 
workforce and the development of effective career pathway systems, 
 
NEAZIWS Workforce Development Board will engage local businesses to gain 
a better understanding of their needs and enlist their assistance in developing

<!-- page 504 -->
96 
 
an integrated workforce system that supports the development of career 
pathways. 
 
This collaboration will make additional resources available among partners and 
ensure that transparent selection of services is provided to Navajo, Apache, 
and Gila County residents. The one-stop partners will work to: 
 
1. Work in unison toward common goals and objectives of the One-Stop 
customer service delivery system. 
 
 
2. Ensure access to career services to all customers 
 
 
3. Meet the needs of the job seekers and business 
 
 
4. Ensure the services and resources meet the needs of an evolving 
economic environment 
 
 
5. Design career pathway systems across partners aligned with business 
demand from pathway to another 
 
 
6. Improve foundational skills to postsecondary education and training for 
customers with barriers to employment 
 
 
7. Assist low-income customers to achieve economic self-sufficiency through 
labor market driven credentials and employment 
 
 
8. Streamline customer service in the one-stop system 
 
The goal of the integrated system is to improve the quality of the workforce, 
reduce welfare dependency, increase economic self-sufficiency, meet skills 
requirements of employers, and enhance the productivity and quality of like of 
the residents of Navajo, Apache, and Gila Counties. 
 
The purpose of the integrative system is to better align the workforce system 
with education and economic development in an effort to create a collective 
response to economic and labor market challenges at the local level. 
 
WIOA Section 121(b)(1)(A) lists the responsibilities of all required partners 
under WIOA.  For consistency, all partners, will assume the responsibilities 
identified below, unless inconsistent with the federal law and regulations that 
authorize the Partners or as otherwise specified in this section. Partner’s 
responsibilities include the following:

<!-- page 505 -->
97 
 
1. Compliance with WIOA and all federal, state, and local laws, regulations, 
rules, policies and plans applicable to parties in their respective roles 
under this MOU and as consistent with the rules that govern each 
Partner’s respective program.  Each Partner expressly agrees to notify 
the LWDB of any changes to the rules governing its respective program 
that impact the Partner’s performance under this MOU.  LWDB will 
communicate the changes to the One-Stop Delivery System and any 
other affected partners. 
 
2. WIOA Section 121(b)(1)(A)(iv) and  §678.420(d) Partners actively 
engage in the operation of the ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS One-Stop 
Customer Service Delivery System in agreement with the stipulations in 
this MOU and with the requirements of authorizing laws. 
 
3. Pursuant to WIOA Section 121(c) Partners must remain as a party of this 
Memorandum of Understanding throughout the Agreement period 
identified in Section 2 of this MOU in order to participate as an 
ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS One-Stop Partner. 
 
4. Career services will be available under the core/ required partners’ 
program to individuals through the ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS One-
Stop Customer Service Delivery System in agreement to this MOU. 
 
5. Provide priority of services to veterans and covered spouses for any 
qualified job training program pursuant to the Jobs for Veterans Act as 
prescribed in 38 U.S. Code §4215. 
 
6. Each partner must ensure compliance by its staff members who work in 
the ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS One-Stop Delivery System with the 
one- stop policies and procedures. Should a conflict exist between the 
ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS One-Stop Delivery System personnel 
policies and partners’ personnel policies, the partners’ policies will 
prevail. 
 
7. Use of common practices and procedures; forms and document; 
software systems or applications; and other forms of media as agreed to 
by all parties in the performance of the ARIZONA@WORK One-Stop 
Delivery System. 
 
8. Appropriately refer customers between the One-Stop Customer Service 
Delivery System and the partners’ agencies for services and activities. 
 
9. Ensure access to services for individuals with barriers to employment, 
including individuals with disabilities. 
 
10. Require cross  training  for  the  One-Stop  Customer  Service  Delivery

<!-- page 506 -->
98 
 
System staff.

<!-- page 507 -->
99 
 
 
V. Sharing Systems and System Access 
 
 
The Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act seek to modernize the 
workforce system to provide comprehensive, integrated and streamlined 
services. Such service delivery will require linking and aligning all 
communication and information sharing amongst the One-Stop Partners. 
Strengthening the linkages between the One-Stop Customer Service Delivery 
System and the Partners’ Programs will be critical to building a system 
designed to support the delivery of these services in Navajo, Apache, and Gila 
counties 
 
 
As per the Workforce Innovation and Opportunity Act, use of the One Stop 
Center will be universal and open to the public.  Individuals utilizing the One 
Stop Center will register in the statewide job match system and may access 
information services and get assistance with resume preparation and job 
search. Individual funding streams will require eligibility documentation for 
program enrollments.  Each funding source will provide their respective 
activities and services with the Adult, Dislocated Worker, and Youth Programs 
enrolling individuals to receive advanced Career Services.  Co-enrollments in 
multiple partner programs are encouraged wherever appropriate and as 
eligibility and other program regulations allow. The object of such co- 
enrollment is to broaden the service options for participants, leverage 
resources, and to respond training, supportive services, and placement support 
needs. 
 
 
The No Wrong Door Approach to providing services through the NEAZIWS 
means one-stop partners will work together to provide services to individuals, 
regardless of their initial contact with partners.  In other words, an individual 
may enter the one-stop, Vocational Rehabilitation, the Adult Education 
Program, or any other partner within the system and will be provided initial 
guidance and direction/referral based on their individual needs. The partners 
agree to work collaboratively to identify individuals with specific needs that may 
be met by system providers and work toward a common in-take and orientation 
process. 
 
 
Shared customers who meet the eligibility requirements of multiple partners will 
be encouraged to co-enroll.  Information regarding common services and/or 
services that support clients from more than one partner provider will be shared 
and clients may receive a level of integrated case management to assure 
optimal services that meet their individual needs. Intake and front office staff

<!-- page 508 -->
100 
 
will provide common information to clients as they attempt to access services 
from any partner.  Staff will receive coordinated training in and about the 
services offered, orientation, eligibility, referral and intake procedures to the 
degree that those services are common and can legally be conducted in a 
common setting.  Outreach and recruitment services will also be a shared 
system service. 
 
Comprehensive One-Stop Center 
 
The Comprehensive One Stop provides workforce services at the 
ARIZONA@WORK Northeastern Arizona Innovative Workforce Solutions 
located: 
 
Gila County Community Services 
5515 S Apache Ave. Suite 200 
Globe, AZ 85501 
 
The One Stop Comprehensive Center is comprised of the local board staff and 
a consortium of entities that, at a minimum, includes four of the required core 
partners identified in WIOA Section 121 (b)(1). The comprehensive center 
provides employment and training services through a vast network of workforce 
experts while also providing business solution services to local employers and 
partners. A variety of services currently offered include: veteran services, on- 
the-job training services, re-entry services, disability resource coordination and 
case management, mini-recruitment and job hiring events, assessments, 
workshops, along with many other services for both job seeker and business 
partners. Core partners providing services at the center include Adult, 
Dislocated Worker, and Youth Programs, Wagner-Peyser Employment 
Services, Adult Education and Literacy, and Vocational Rehabilitation. The 
additional partners providing services at the center include: Section 8 Housing, 
Community Action Program, Weatherization, Minor Home Repair, Gila 
Employment and Special Training. The business hours of operation are 8:00 
a.m. to 5:00 p.m. Monday through Friday. 
 
 
Affiliate Sites 
 
Adult, Dislocated Worker, and Youth Programs and Employment services are 
available to job seekers and employers at the Payson, Winslow, Show Low, 
and Eagar sites. The business hours are Monday through Friday, 8:00 a.m. to 
5:00 p.m. The location of the affiliate sites are: provided: 
 
 
 
Payson DES Office 
100 Tonto Street, Suite 200 
Payson, AZ 85541

101 
 
 
Show Low affiliate site partners providing services include:  Adult, Dislocated 
Worker, and Youth Programs, Vocational Rehabilitation, and Family 
Assistance.

319 E. 3rd Street 
Winslow, AZ 86047 
 
Winslow affiliate site partners providing services include: Adult, Dislocated 
Worker, and Youth Programs, Vocational Rehabilitation, and Family 
Assistance. 
 
Show Low DES Office 
2500 E Cooley Street, Suite 410 
Show Low, AZ 85901 
 
Show Low affiliate site partners providing services include: Adult, Dislocated 
Worker, and Youth Programs, Vocational Rehabilitation, Family Assistance, 
and Division of Developmental Disability. 
 
Eagar/Springerville DES Office 
74 N. Main Street, Suite 6 
Eagar, AZ 85925

Assistance. 
 
Access to Technology 
 
Technology plays a particularly important role in the delivery of services in the 
NEAZIWS, because of the geographic area to be covered within the three 
counties.  Partners will share, as appropriate, intake, assessment reporting and 
data collection via agreed upon electronic methodologies.  Common training in 
the use of technology and confidentiality of information will be conducted for all 
partners. 
 
 
WIOA emphasizes technology as a critical tool for making all aspects of 
information exchange possible, including client tracking, common case 
management, reporting, and data collection. To support the use of these tools 
each Partner agrees to the following: 
 
 
1.  Comply with the applicable provision of WIOA Title I, Rehabilitation Act, and 
any other appropriate statues or requirements;

<!-- page 510 -->
102 
 
 
2.  The principal of common reports and shared information through electronic 
mechanisms, including shared data and technology; 
 
 
3.  Commit to share information to the greatest extent allowable under their 
governing legislation and confidentiality requirements; 
 
 
4.  Maintain all records of customers or local Partners (e.g. applications, 
eligibility and referral records, or any other individual records related to 
services provided under this MOU) in the strictest confidence, and use them 
solely for purposes directly related to such services; 
 
 
5.  Develop technological enhancements that allow interfaces of common 
information needs, as appropriate; and 
 
 
6.  Understand that system security provisions shall be agreed upon by all 
local Partners. 
 
 
Arizona Job Connection (AJC) 
 
 
The LWDB and the partners will jointly utilize the common Arizona Job 
Connection System approved jointly by the Workforce Arizona Council and the 
State Administrative Entity. Where a One-Stop partner utilizes the proprietary 
Arizona Job Connection system, shared information and data agreements will 
be utilized to support access to information between the partners as well as for 
intake and referral of customers and recording elements as allowed by 
authorizing law and regulations. 
 
Job Orders 
 
Employers can contact any Employment Services Representative at any 
location to request to have their job posted in the Arizona Job Connection 
(AJC) system. The process for employers posting job orders has been 
streamlined and employers can post job orders through the Employment 
Services Representative via the web site.   Employers can also download the 
AJC job order application and post a job orders in AJC. 
 
Referral System 
 
The LWDB is working with partners to develop a joint client Referral System. 
The purpose of the referral process is to provide timely and effective support 
that initiates that an appropriate plan of action for clients is initiated and 
properly tracks the clients’ referrals to community partners. The contribution of

<!-- page 511 -->
103 
 
all partners in the development of this system will increase quality service 
delivery and support in order for clients to receive timely and relevant services 
to meet their individual needs. The client referral system will be the central 
component for referring customers between partners. 
 
 
Customer information will also be shared within the limits of individual release 
of information forms to help reduce the redundancy in data and information 
collection and present a common unified approach for clients. 
 
Access for Individuals with Barriers to Employment 
 
All partners agree to prioritize services as outlined by the WIOA and for 
individuals with barriers to employment. WIOA Section 3(24) defines the term 
“individual with a barrier to employment” as a member of one (1) or more of the 
following populations: 
 
 
1.  Displaced Homemakers 
2.  Low-income individuals 
3.  Indian, Alaskan Native, and Native Hawaiians 
4.  Individuals with disabilities 
5.  Older individuals 
6.  Ex-offenders 
7.  Homeless individuals 
8.  Youth who are in or have aged out of the foster care system 
9.  Individuals who are English Language learners, individuals who have 
low levels of literacy, and individuals facing substantial cultural barriers 
10. Eligible migrant and seasonal farm workers 
11. Individuals within 1 year of exhausting lifetime eligibility (for TANF) for 
the state of Arizona. (Note: federal government has instituted a 5 year 
time lime and the State of Arizona a 12 month limit) 
12. Single parents (including single pregnant women) 
13. Long-term unemployed individuals 
14. Other groups as the Governor determines to have barriers to 
employment 
 
Partners commit to offer priority of services to recipients of public assistance, 
other low-income individuals, or individuals who are basic skills deficient when 
providing career services, education and training services. Barriers to 
employment status is established at the time of eligibility or enrollment and do 
not change during the period of participation. 
 
 
Veterans and eligible spouses continue to receive priority of service among all 
eligible individuals; however, they must meet the WIOA adult program eligibility 
criteria and meet the criteria under WIOA Section 134(c)(3)(E).

<!-- page 512 -->
104 
 
Title II:  Adult Education and Literacy 
 
The Northeastern Arizona Workforce Development Area Title II provider(s) offer 
adult basic education classes for individuals in need of their High School 
Equivalency (HSE) diploma.  Classes are aligned with the Arizona College and 
Career Readiness Standards. Programs utilize initial assessment information 
via the Test of Adult Basic Education (TABE).  Individuals are placed in classes 
that align with their academic ability and with consideration for their individual 
time schedules. 
 
Along with the other core program partners, Adult Education and Literacy in 
Navajo, Apache, and Gila counties has agreed to the customer flow that 
provides for a “no wrong door” approach to serving individuals in the 
Northeastern Arizona Workforce Development Area. The design has all core 
program partner clients registering in the ARIZONA@WORK database and 
viewing a system orientation as well as receiving any basic career services 
available at the location they access. Adult Education and Literacy has also 
agreed to the referral process and initial screening to identify potential services 
for all American Job Center jobseekers.” (Northeastern Arizona Workforce 
Development Business Plan) 
 
Adult Education and Literacy providers in the Northeastern Arizona Workforce 
Development Area are Northland Pioneer College (Apache & Navajo Counties) 
and Gila County Regional School District (Gila County). 
 
 
Services provided by these providers include some/all of the following: 
Pre-intake Registration 
Orientation Referral to other partners 
Pre/post assessment Academic skills instruction 
Variety of delivery modalities: 
Distance Education 
Face-to Face classes 
Hybrid options 
Day/Evening classes 
 
Title IV Vocational Rehabilitation 
 
Vocational Rehabilitation (VR) program will offer opportunities for local 
workforce development staff to participate in training specific to VR program 
information, disability awareness, disability etiquette and culture, as well as 
other disability specific trainings which might be offered through the VR 
program. Increased knowledge of disability awareness, etiquette, and available 
resources and services will assist career center staff in becoming comfortable 
with assisting individuals with disabilities when they access center services. 
Other technical assistance information can be provided to the One-Stop center

<!-- page 513 -->
105 
 
staff includes information on Social Security Work Benefits, Americans with 
Disability Act, and Section 503 compliance. VR staff will participate in One-Stop 
training in order to facilitate cooperation and coordination between parties. 
 
VR is committed to assisting the One-Stop center ensure programmatic and 
physical access to afford equal, effective, and meaningful access to center 
services to individuals with disabilities. VR staff will work cooperatively with the 
center staff to determine the assistive technology needs of the center in order to 
allow individuals with disabilities to access services in the One-Stop center and 
participate alongside other job seekers. One-Stop center staff will be trained by 
VR on the use of the assistive technology. 
 
In order to facilitate access to system services, VR staff will be available on 
Mondays from 8:30 a.m-12 noon p.m. each week, excluding holidays. VR staff 
will work cooperatively with One-Stop staff in order to facilitate reciprocal 
referrals and joint planning. Client data, such as demographic information and 
evaluation and testing results, can be shared between partners with client 
approval to assist in reciprocal referrals and joint service planning. VR and One- 
Stop services can be used as comparable benefits for mutually eligible clients. 
 
Vocational Rehabilitation continues to engage with adult education and join 
technical education districts to facilitate unique learning opportunities for 
individuals with disabilities that lead to certification and credentials necessary to 
obtain employment. VR will collaborate with the local board in an effort to build 
career pathways and educational opportunities which meet the needs of the 
local economy. 
 
Performance Accountability 
 
All partners agree to meet quarterly to exchange information about performance 
goals and the attainment of the goals. Partners will develop strategies to assist 
each other in meeting the respective performance goals. 
 
Confidentiality 
 
Partners agree to share customer information only for the benefit and with the 
expressed and informed consent of the participant and, if applicable, the 
participant’s parent or guardian. 
 
Supervision 
 
Each partner agency will be responsible for supervising its own staff formally. 
Gila County Community Services in its role as Lead One-Stop Operator will be 
responsible for day-to-day functional supervision of the comprehensive Center 
and may work with partner agencies to ensure the reception desk and resource 
room have coverage that is shared by partners when necessary.

<!-- page 514 -->
106 
 
 
Dispute Resolution Process 
 
It is expected partners will participate in decision-making by consensus.  In 
instances where consensus cannot be reached at the lowest level and service 
delivery for the One-Stop System is impaired, those One-Stop partners who 
are parties to the dispute shall submit to the following dispute resolution 
procedure if state or federal laws and regulations allow: 
 
A. If the partners are unable to resolve a dispute to the satisfaction of the 
members who are parties to the dispute, the complaint shall be 
submitted in writing to the Northeastern Arizona Workforce Development 
Board’s One-Stop System Committee within 15 days of the initial 
dispute. 
 
B. The Committee shall evaluate the merits of the dispute and may attempt 
to resolve the dispute through mediation. However, in all cases, the 
Committee shall prepare a response to the complaint within 30 days. 
 
C. If any party to the dispute is not satisfied with the decision of the 
Committee, the dispute shall be referred to a five person-hearing panel, 
chosen by the LWDB, for resolution. The panel shall be requesting to 
make a determination within 30 days. The decision of the panel shall be 
final and binding on all parties to the dispute. 
 
The NEAZIWS Deputy Director has the responsibility to review each complaint 
in accordance with local, state, and federal guidelines and ensure appropriate 
action is taken. The NEAZIWS Deputy Director will ensure all partners are 
properly trained in the procedures for partner dispute resolution. 
 
Complaint Procedures 
 
Any partner staff can receive a customer complaint. Phone calls, faxes, e- 
mails, and letters are forms of customer complaints: 
 
A. Grievances, Complaints and Appeals 
1.  A grievance or complaint is a written description of an alleged 
violation of the WIOA, dated and signed by an interested party. An 
appeal is any written, dated, and signed communication by an 
interested party expressing their intent to appeal any action or 
inaction with respect to a grievance or complaint to a higher level. 
Any action pursuant to the original decision is stayed until a final 
decision is made by the State. 
2.  The freedom to file a complaint or appeal may not be limited or 
interfered with in any way.

<!-- page 515 -->
107 
 
3.  Of interested parties in connection with complaints or appeals may 
not be limited or interfered with in any way. 
4.  The local area will make reasonable efforts to assure that the 
information will be understood by affected participants and other 
individuals, including youth and those who are limited-English 
speaking individuals. Such efforts will comply with the language 
requirements specified in 29 CFR 37.35 regarding the provision of 
services and information in languages other than English. 
 
B. Right to Grieve, Complain or Appeal 
1.  Any interested person has a right to file a grievance or complaint. 
2.  Any complaint relating to a term or condition of employment shall be 
filed under the employer’s established grievance procedures. 
3.  All other complaints shall be initiated at the recipient level. 
 
C. Time Limitations 
1.  Complaints under this section shall be filed within one year of the 
alleged occurrence. 
 
D. Time Computation, Filing and Receipt 
1.  For purposes of these procedures, any compliant, appeal, 
applications, request, notice, objection, petition, report or other 
information is considered received and filed timely: 
2.  If transmitted via the United States Postal Service on the date it is 
mailed as shown by the postmark, or in the absence of a postmark 
the postage meter mark, on the envelope in which it is received; or 
3.  If not postmark or postage meter marked, or if the mark is illegible, 
on the date it is actually received. 
4.  If transmitted by any means other than the United States Postal 
Service on the date it is actually received. 
5.  If a delay in filing is due to an error or misinformation by the 
organizations or person with whom the complaint or appeal is filed, 
the time limitation shall be adjusted in a manager equitable to the 
complainant. 
6.  Any reference to “days” is interpreted as calendar days.  In 
computing any period of time, the date of the act, event or default is 
not included. The last day of the period computed is counted, unless 
it is a Saturday, a Sunday, or a legal holiday, in which case the 
period runs until the end of the next day which is not a Saturday, a 
Sunday, or a legal holiday. 
 
E. Hearings 
1.  An impartial Hearing Office will hear complaints at the local level.

<!-- page 516 -->
108 
 
Indemnification 
 
 
The Parties intend and agree that they should each be responsible for their 
own acts, errors and omissions, and the acts, errors and omissions of their own 
employees, agents, clients, guests, invitees, or persons acting at the direction 
or under the control of that Party. Neither Party to this Agreement agrees to 
defend, indemnify, or hold harmless the other Party from liability hereunder. 
However, if the common law or a statute provides for either a right to indemnity 
and/or a right to contribution to any party to this agreement, then the right to 
pursue one or both of these remedies is preserved. 
 
Disabilities Services 
 
Pursuant to 29 CFR Part 37.7 through 37.10, the parties to this agreement 
will assure that the following is provided, to the extent possible, in the One- 
Stop delivery system. 
 
A. One-Stop center facilities that are programmatically and architecturally 
accessible; 
 
B. Program access for individuals with disabilities; 
 
C. Reasonable accommodations for individuals with disabilities. 
 
Ensure all parties to this Memorandum of Understanding agree to abide by the 
provisions outlined in the Workforce Investment Act Section 188 
“Nondiscrimination”. 
 
VI. Shared System Services an d Shared Customers 
 
The ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS one stop system partners will collaborate to 
provide physical and/or electronic access to shared customers and, for enrolled 
services, to Adult, Dislocated Workers, and Youth, either by customer contact, 
or established relationship from a partner program. 
 
Arizona@ Work NEAZIWS one stop centers are a central point of entry to 
employment and training programs for an individual, without regard to eligibility; 
for information on employment opportunities, career options, labor market 
information; and workforce development services and programs provided by 
strategic partners. The ARIZONA@WORK NEAZIWS integrated service 
delivery system design allows for the parties to this MOU to deliver shared and 
complementary services in collaboration with the one stop centers to reach 
more job seekers. Sector-focused strategies will increase access to viable 
career pathways to better prepare job seekers for in-demand occupations in 
high growth industries.

<!-- page 517 -->
109 
 
VII. Data Access 
 
Records of common clients who receive services funded through Wagner- 
Peyser, Title 1B or other USDOL funded employment programs, who also 
receive services from a partner program, will be maintained in a workforce 
case management and reporting system provided by the Department of 
Economic Security. 
 
Partners who wish to obtain access to workforce case management and 
reporting system may do so under terms and conditions of this Memorandum 
of Understanding if the following conditions are met: 
a.   The partner is responsible for all costs associated with data access. 
b.   The partner agrees to adhere to state and local policies governing 
confidentiality, data usage, and standards for data entry 
c.   Each partner staff member who requires access will follow the security 
access procedures established by the Department of Economic Security 
and sign the appropriate data access forms prior to receiving access. 
 
VIII. Resource Sharing and Infrastructure Costs 
 
Partners agree to base cost sharing on direct square footage used for each 
respective partner with dedicated space in the One Stop Center(s) and a 
corresponding share of the common area costs and share the cost of the 
receptionist position as based on percentage defined in Cost Sharing 
Agreement. Partners that do not have dedicated space in the One Stop Center 
will negotiate with the administrative entity to determine an appropriate 
financial or in-kind arrangement for cost sharing.   The current cost sharing 
spreadsheet is attached to this document. Addendum I outlines, based on 
square footage, the lease amounts for the Comprehensive One Stop partners. 
Addendum 2 outlines the salary and ere cost allocation for the Comprehensive 
One Stop reception position shared by the One Stop partners. Payment by 
partners will be made within thirty days from the first of the month due to the 
Gila County Community Services.

Gila River Indian Community Grant Application

Narrative 
Please structure your proposal to provide the following information in the order indicated.  Provide the narrative in 
paragraph form in the text field provided.  Please be thorough but strive for brevity. 
1. Briefly describe the organization’s history, mission and goals. 
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District is a Fire District providing fire, EMS and ambulance transport service 
to citizens in unincorporated Navajo County. The District provides ambulance service to a 200 square 
mile around the communities of Heber-Overgaard and for 55 miles of State Highway. 
2. If the applying organization is non-municipal or a non-profit 501c, please describe your relationship with Arizona 
municipalities including past and present support, whether it be through partnerships, collaborations, in-kind 
contributions, grants, tax-based funding or other.  Describe the supporting municipality’s role in this project.  If 
the applying organization is a municipality governmental sub-division please enter the text “Not applicable.” 
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District is a Fire District located in the communities of Heber-Overgaard in 
Navajo County. Navajo County collects property tax revenues for the Fire District and maintains the bank 
account for the District. The Fire District partners with Navajo County on many projects. The Heber-
Overgaard Fire District is currently working on a joint grant project with Navajo County for fuels 
reduction in the community through AZ Department of Forestry and Fire Management.  
3. Describe the proposed project and planned goals and activities. 
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District operates the ambulance service for the area and needs to replace a 
2008 Dodge ambulance which has reached the end of it’s service life. The District will purchase a new 
Type II ambulance to service the area. 
4. Describe how the proposed project satisfies one or more of the priority funding areas identified by the Gila River 
Indian Community. 
The Heber-Overgaard Fire Distirct provides Advanced Life Support ambulance service to a 200 square 
mile area including 55 miles of State highway. ALS service and transport to a hospital is a key component 
of Public Safety Service. 
5. Identify the needs/problems to be addressed, target population and number of people to be served by the 
project. 
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District ambulance response area is 200 square miles and includes the 
unincorporated communities of Heber and Overgaard. The permanent population of Heber-Overgaard is 
approximately 3500, however the seasonal population can swell to 15000 in the summer due to the 
location of many summer homes for Phoenix residents. The District ambulance response area also 
covers 55 miles of AZ State highways (SR260, SR277 and SR377). This highway corridor is the main route

<!-- page 524 -->
Gila River Indian Community Grant Application 
Grant Cycle 2017 
 
from Phoenix to I40 for travel to and from the eastern United States. A large number of people and 
commerce use this route each day increasing the population served significantly. The Fire Station is 
located in the center of the District and is 40 miles from the closest hospital causing extreme wear and 
tear on our ambulances. Our oldest ambulance (2008 Dodge) has 130,000 miles and is experiencing 
mechanical issues. Due to changes in Fire District finance caused by Prop 117 the District is having a 
difficult time with capital replacement costs due to shrinking property tax base and rising costs. 
6. Define the project as a new or continuing program. 
Providing ambulance service to the Heber-Overgaard Fire District CON is a continuing program. 
7. Provide a brief timeline including start and finish dates.  Indicate if the timeline is flexible. 
Upon approval of the grant the Fire District will request bids for a Type II ambulance that meets the 
specifications of the Fire District. The ambulance will be purchased and placed in service within 6 
months of receiving the grant. 
8. Identify other organizations, partners or funders participating in the project and their roles. 
Navajo County will be the fiscal agent for this grant. As an Arizona Fire District under Title 48 the County 
manages our funds. Navajo County would sponser the Heber-Overgaard Fire District in the grant 
process, receive the funds and transfer the funds to the Fire District’s bank account which is managed by 
the County. The Heber-Overgaard Fire District would manage the grant, execute the purchase and 
complete all necessary grant reports. 
9. Would you be able to implement the proposed plan if your organization received partial funding for this project? 
Yes 
10. Describe your plan for project sustainability beyond the grant period.  If this is a program/project previously 
funded by the Gila River Indian Community describe efforts made towards the previously described 
sustainability plan. 
The ambulance will be operated and maintained by the Fire District. The ambulance will provide service 
for 7-10 years. Heber-Overgaard Fire District will pay for ongoing maintenance of the ambulance. 
11. Describe your plan to document progress and results. 
The Heber-Overgaard Fire District would maintain a grant file for this project and complete the 
necessary grant reporting to the Gila River Indian Community. 
12. Indicate any application to and/or awards made by a tribe other than the Gila River Indian Community for state 
shared revenues for this and any other project for the past five (5) years.  If this information is included in a 
separate attachment, please indicate that here. 
None

Board of Supervisors Regular 6.a.        
Meeting Date: 03/14/2017  
Title: Share Hope Initiative Presentation
Submitted For: Arno Hall, Director of Juvenile Court Services 
Submitted By: Laura Mudge, Chief Financial Officer
Department: Probation Division: Juvenile Probation
Presented By: Arno Hall and Lynda Wilson
Motion before the Board:
Presentation of Share Hope Initiative.
Background:
The presentation includes information about the transition of Juvenile Justice in Arizona moving
from Best Practices to Evidence-based Practices and the implementation of the Share Hope
Initiative in Navajo County, which promotes communication between stakeholders, and reduces
recidivism by juvenile offenders.
 
Recommendation: 
Expand the Share Hope Initiative as proposed by the Navajo County Juvenile Probation
Department.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6.b.        
Meeting Date: 03/14/2017  
Title: Family Counseling Funding/County Match
Submitted For: Arno Hall, Director of Juvenile Court Services 
Submitted By: Laura Mudge, Chief Financial Officer
Department: Probation Division: Juvenile Probation
Presented By: Arno Hall
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 06-17, approving the Family
Counseling Grant budget modification to increase FY17 funding.
Background:
This is a renewable grant that has been established for thirty (30) + years which pays juvenile
counseling for families. If not approved the County would lose the additional funding from the
state and would not have money for family counseling for juveniles.
 
Recommendation: 
It is the recommendation that the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, which approved the
County match of $2,996 at the May 24, 2016 board meeting, approve an increased match to
$5,706 to the Juvenile Probation Department so they may receive the Family Counseling Grant
from the Administrative Office of the Courts increase from $15,587 to $29,687 for the 16/17
Fiscal Year.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 17
Budgeted Y/N: N
Amount Requested:$2,710
Fiscal Impact:
The additional $2,710 in county match allows the Family Counseling state grant to increase it's
allocation to the county by $14,100 for FY17.  These funds are used for our Juvenile counseling
costs.  Without these Family Counseling funds the county would be responsible for 100% of these
treatment costs.
Original Family Counseling request on May 24, 2016
State Allocation = $15,587; County Match = $2,996
MidYear Adjustment
Additional State Allocation = $14,100; Additional county match = $2,710
Total FY17 Allocations:

<!-- page 538 -->
State Allocation = $29,687; County match = $5,706

Board of Supervisors Regular 7.a.        
Meeting Date: 03/14/2017  
Title: Award of Contract B17-01-018 Supply and Delivery of Cattle Guards
Submitted For: Glenn Kephart, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Glenn Kephart
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of contract #B17-01-018 Supply and Delivery of Cattle Guards to
Oldcastle Precast, Inc. and W W Cattle Guards, LLC effective March 25, 2017.
Background:
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 2:00 p.m. M.S.T, March 1, 2017, in strict accordance with the
specifications on file in the office of the Navajo County Public Works.  No bids were accepted
after 2:00 p.m. M.S.T.  The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 2:15 p.m.  This is a
two-year "multi-award" contract for cattle guards which are purchased based on price,
convenience, availability and/or quality.  The award of contracts to all bidders gives us the
flexibility to utilize a second vendor should the lowest bidder not be available.
 
Recommendation: 
The unit prices have increased $50 to $200 dollars depending on the unit size from the contract
two years ago.  After comparing prices with other agencies the increased prices are in line with
todays market; therefore we are recommending award of contract to both Oldcastle Precast, Inc.
and W W Cattle Guards, LLC.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year:
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
The Public Works budget includes funding for Road Maintenance materials.

C-1
CONTRACT
THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into as of March 25, 2017 (the
“Effective Date”) by and between Navajo County (“County”) and Oldcastle Precast, Inc.
(“Contractor”).
1. SCOPE OF WORK: Contractor shall furnish any and all plant materials, labor, 
hauling and construction equipment and services required for performing all work 
for Supply & Delivery of Cattle Guards, Contract # B17-01-018, in accordance 
with the Contract Documents, in a workmanlike manner to the satisfaction of 
County through its Engineers.
2. CONTRACT DOCUMENTS: The Contract Documents comprise this Contract 
and the attached ⾙ Call for Bids, ☐ Specifications, ☐ General Provisions, ⾙
Special Provisions, ⾙ Technical Specification, ⾙ Proposal dated February 28, 
2017, ⾙ Bidding Schedule, ⾙ Bid Bond, ☐ Performance Bond, ⾙ No Collusion 
Affidavit, ⾙ Subcontracting Certification, ☐ Plans, and addenda thereto, if any.  
If there is any conflict in the documents, this Contract shall control over all others; 
the Specifications shall control over the General Provisions and Special 
Provisions; and the Special Provisions shall control over the General Provisions.
3. NO LIENS / TIME FOR COMPLETION: Contractor shall furnish all materials, 
equipment and labor in accordance with the Contract Documents free and clear 
of all claims, liens and charges whatsoever, within the time, or times, stated in 
the Proposal.
4. CANCELLATION: This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 
38-511, (concerning conflicts of interest).
5. PAYMENTS:  Payment shall be made on the basis of the unit price(s) bid for the 
awarded items in the Bidding Schedule for Contract # B17-01-018.  Contractor 
shall submit invoices to County for all work performed hereunder.  No later than 
30 days after each invoice has been verified and approved by the Engineer, 
County shall pay to Contractor 100% of the approved amount.
6. CERTIFICATIONS :
A. Pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-4401, Contractor and its subcontractors warrant 
compliance with all federal immigration laws and regulations relating to their 
employees and compliance with the E-verify requirements under A.R.S. § 23-
214(A). Contractor's or any subcontractor's breach of this warranty shall be 
deemed a material breach of the Contract and may result in termination of the 
Contract by County. County may randomly inspect the books and records of 
Contractor and its subcontractors to verify compliance with this warranty.

<!-- page 545 -->
C-2
Contractor and its subcontractors warrant to keep their books and records 
open for random inspection by County during normal business hours.
B. Pursuant to A.R.S. §§ 35-391.06 and 35-393.06, Contractor certifies that it 
does not have a scrutinized business operation in Sudan or Iran. The term 
“scrutinized business operation” shall have the meaning set forth in A.R.S. §§ 
35-391 or 35-393, as applicable. Any falsification in regard to this warranty 
shall be deemed a material breach of the Contract and may result in

Board of Supervisors Regular 7.b.        
Meeting Date: 03/14/2017  
Title: Award of Contract B17-01-017 Supply of Various Roadway Materials
Submitted For: Glenn Kephart, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Glenn Kephart
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of contract B17-01-017 Various Roadway Materials to Perkins Cinders,
Inc., Hatch Construction and Paving, Inc., Brimhall Sand and Rock and Building Materials, Inc.,
and Cholla Redy Mix, Inc.
Background:
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Governmental
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m. M.S.T, March 1, 2017, in strict accordance with the
specifications on file in the office of the Navajo County Public Works.  No bids were accepted
after 3:00 p.m. M.S.T.  The bids were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 p.m.  This is a
annual "multi-award" contract for roadway materials which are purchased based on price,
convenience, availability and/or quality.  The award of contracts to all bidders allows adequate
coverage for road materials by location.
Recommendation: 
The unit prices have decreased approximately .64 cents per ton for all material with the exception
of 2 sack slurry and concrete which has an average increase of $3.17 per cubic yard.  The 2 sack
slurry and concrete materials are not utilized as often as the other materials and with these being
our local vendors, staff is recommending award of contract to all bidders.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year:
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
The Public Works budget includes funding for road maintenance material.

C-1
CONTRACT
THIS CONTRACT is made and entered into as of March 28, 2017 (the
“Effective Date”) by and between Navajo County (“County”) and Brimhall Sand & Rock 
& Building Materials, Inc. (“Contractor”).
1. SCOPE OF WORK: Contractor shall furnish any and all materials, equipment and 
labor required for performing all work for Supplying Various Roadway Materials, 
Contract # B17-01-017 , in accordance with the Contract Documents, in a 
workmanlike manner to the satisfaction of County through its Engineers.
2. CONTRACT DOCUMENTS: The Contract Documents comprise this Contract and 
the attached ⾙ Call for Bids, ☐ Specifications, ☐ General Provisions, ⾙ Special 
Provisions, ⾙ Proposal dated February 23, 2017, ⾙ Bidding Schedule, ⾙ Bid Bond, 
☐ Performance Bond, ⾙ No Collusion Affidavit, ⾙ Subcontracting Certification, ☐
Plans, and ⾙ addenda thereto, if any.  If there is any conflict in the documents, this 
Contract shall control over all others; the Specifications shall control over the 
General Provisions and Special Provisions; and the Special Provisions shall control 
over the General Provisions.
3. NO LIENS / TIME FOR COMPLETION: Contractor shall furnish all materials, 
equipment and labor in accordance with the Contract Documents free and clear of 
all claims, liens and charges whatsoever, within the time, or times, stated in the 
Proposal.
4. CANCELLATION: This Contract is subject to cancellation pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-
511, (concerning conflicts of interest).
5. PAYMENTS:   Payment shall be made on the basis of the unit price(s) bid for the 
awarded items in the Bidding Schedule for Contract # B17-01-017.  Contractor shall 
submit invoices to County for all work performed hereunder.  No later than 30 days 
after each invoice has been verified and approved by the Engineer, County shall pay 
to Contractor 100% of the approved amount.
6. CERTIFICATIONS :
A. Pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-4401, Contractor and its subcontractors warrant 
compliance with all federal immigration laws and regulations relating to their 
employees and compliance with the E-verify requirements under A.R.S. § 23-
214(A). Contractor's or any subcontractor's breach of this warranty shall be 
deemed a material breach of the Contract and may result in termination of the 
Contract by County. County may randomly inspect the books and records of 
Contractor and its subcontractors to verify compliance with this warranty.  
Contractor and its subcontractors warrant to keep their books and records open 
for random inspection by County during normal business hours.

<!-- page 553 -->
C-2
B. Pursuant to A.R.S. §§ 35-391.06 and 35-393.06, Contractor certifies that it 
does not have a scrutinized business operation in Sudan or Iran. The term 
“scrutinized business operation” shall have the meaning set forth in A.R.S. §§ 
35-391 or 35-393, as applicable. Any falsification in regard to this warranty shall 
be deemed a material breach of the Contract and may result in termination of 
the Contract by County
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the authorized representatives of the parties have 
executed this Contract as of the Effective Date.
